# Library build blocker tracker

Latest semantic checkpoint: [source Unsize upcasts](pa31-source-object-upcasts.md)
supports non-quantified intrinsic object upcasts with full retained debts.
Ten PA31 outcomes, seven private controls, bounded diamond/noise work and three
paired native observers pass. The retained core table had17 upcast fences;
whole-core verification remains pending behind its observer timeout. Add
incremental census progress next, preserving compiler quotas and observer ceilings.

panic. Focused private/public/native evidence passes. Full predecessor42800653535

Execution follows the [core census and transport plan](core-census-plan.md)

Lane D [record variants](parallel/record-variants.md), item (i), carries

The declaration row keeps kind Variant and names a record variant's
type-namespace definition. Each consumer re-derives the namespace from the

- `construct` and the `InteriorNul` client exit 42 natively. Four opponents

- With (ii), a record variant's zero-slot fn field exports and forms in its

  (ii)'s record-variant opponent now export, and two fresh-consumer
  witnesses that call through the field exit 42 natively, as rustc's do.
  (ii)'s `ArgumentType` opponent keeps the higher-ranked fence, because its
  formatter's `&mut` is late-bound.
- Among existing inputs, the flip survey against `198fdea9b` changes nothing.
  Enum-match's forged shape-byte-2 row moves to byte 3 (record-variant
  amendment 3, written after the run that observed it).
- Not in the packet: `PartialEq`, because an exported `==` needs comparison
  and `Not` role transport, its own item. A method call through an imported
  `Clone` impl stays "no applicable method for this receiver", and an exported
  match on a unit-variant value stays "source place transport supports locals
  and reference projections only" (amendment 2). Both are pre-existing
  rejects-valid.
- core 1.98 has two record-variant enums. `FromBytesWithNulError`'s header,
  construction and Clone and Copy derives are in reach. `ArgumentType`'s
  formatter, `unsafe fn(NonNull<()>, &mut Formatter<'_>) -> Result`, is
  higher-ranked and waits for HR.

Lane C [unsafe associated functions](parallel/guarded-methods.md) lets a
version-13 export carry `unsafe` inherent methods, trait members and impl
members, and the unsafe direct and pointer calls inside or to them, with no
wire change. Under version 13 an associated function's `unsafe` no longer
needs a wider profile, its original and public safety must agree, and an impl
member's safety must be its trait member's. Calls follow direct Call's
matrix, which each consumer re-proves.

- 16 clients exit 42 natively, including `Argument::fmt`'s shape, an unsafe
  method of a generic impl, one with a late region and one of a
  lifetime-capturing impl, a const unsafe method in a client const, an unsafe
  trait member called through a bound and implemented by the client, and a
  reified unsafe associated fn. A safe call with no unsafe block is
  rejected, as rustc rejects it (E0133).
- The four approved movers export: const-methods' `const unsafe fn get`
  (M1), F's target-feature `Lane::get` with its returned `inherent` client
  (M2 and M2a), R2's `H::call` (M3) and (ii)'s `Table::go` (M4).
- Kept fences, by name: an unsafe `extern "C"` method keeps the nonordinary
  text and an unsafe trait default keeps the provided-body text, both known
  rejects-valid. A trait's unsafe method implemented safely, and the reverse,
  are rejected by RustGM's own frontend, as rustc rejects them (E0053).
- Versions 12 and 10, and the base reader, keep "source nonordinary callable
  effect/safety/convention requires a wider profile" and "source unsafe
  associated Calls require their wider actual profile".
- Among existing inputs the flip survey (1,929 runs) changes only M1 to M4,
  under version 13. The 78 compared packets are byte-identical.
- **`SliceIndex`** (`slice/index.rs:149`) is a const unsafe trait, so it also
  needs const trait impls.
- **Recorded:** rustc 1.98.0 ICEs on M1's declared client at a match over a
  provider that implements `Copy` for no primitive; the approved fixture
  amendment returns `N as i32` (amendment 1). Primitive-impl unsafe methods,
  such as `u8::unchecked_add`, follow from the rule but are untested and not
  claimed.

- Method lookup now reads callable sugar that names no lifetime, an empty

- **f01, prerequisite of bound-region lookup** (tracked accepts-invalid, census batch 21

- Tracked rejects-valid, low priority (probe `f03a`): a const trait with a

  FnMut(u8) -> u8 {}`, is rejected with "ambiguous trait assumptions in

- Tracked accepts-invalid (probe `g01`): `[const]` bounds on an inherent
  impl's parameters, such as `impl<T, U, F: [const] FnMut(T) -> U> S<T, F>`,
  are accepted, where rustc 1.98.0 rejects them ("`[const]` is not allowed

- Census 24's record 1 is the next lead.

Lane B [item B, negative premises in nested trait proofs](parallel/negative-nested-proofs.md)
carries the other header's Sized and `MetaSized` premises into every nested
trait proof of a negative coherence query and retires the projected-size
fence:

- Repaired: the known rejects-valid `nested-projected-size` and its swapped
  twin. A projected-size nominal's size is now decided by the proof's trait
  State under the same premises: it normalizes the projected tail under the
  set, and decides a rigid alias by the set and its own declared bound.
  `nested-projected-premise-side` and its swapped twin are rejected with the
  overlap text, as rustc rejects them with E0119.
- 14 new twins were declared before their rustc 1.98.0 oracle. Of the 61
  cases, all but two probe pairs match rustc.
- Fixed before landing: a trait State started inside a placeholder walk took
  its premises from the proof's shapes slot, which the walk had taken, so its
  nested proofs ran premise-less and read the representative's declaration.
  The walk now passes its own set. A sweep found no other holder that reads
  its set from a slot that can be empty.
- Open rejects-valid for item C: the `probe-projection-premise` pair, which
  rustc accepts. P's formation goal `V: Tr` fails before any Sized goal,
  because the other header's `U: Tr<Out = u8>` is a trait premise, which this
  item does not assume. The `probe-trait-premise` pair stays open for the
  same reason.
- The [nested proof checkpoint](parallel/nested-proof-reuse.md) repairs the
  repeated projected-size suffix work and its omitted nested accounting. The
  measured normalization count changes from 2^d-1 to d through 31 for both truth
  values and premise modes. All child work is paid once; original work limits
  and a named actual-owner depth guard remain live. Generic depths 31/32 and
  closed 12/31 are accepted on the default development CLI stack. The earlier
  predicted nominal-depth 32 rejection was disproved and is not imposed.
- Remaining performance debt: total accounted work is quadratic on these
  controls, with larger metadata/tail work for false results. The proposed
  affine bound failed and remains open. Immutable development A/A+ABBA passes;
  matching optimized measurement and release-stack qualification remain pending.
- Known rejects-valid: canonical selection for
  `Field<U>(for<'a> fn(P^d<&'a U>), u8)` at d>=2. Body-inference Cursor owners
  still bound child work locally without summing it into their own ledger;
  dispatch normalization needs the same ownership audit. Neither follow-up is
  represented as completed by this checkpoint.

Lane C [zero-slot nominal callable fields](parallel/callable-fields.md), (ii),
lets a version-13 export carry a struct's or tuple variant's closed Rust fn
field, with no wire change. Version 13 admits a callable-type binder that a
nominal owns, with no parent and no slots. Each consumer forms the field in
that nominal's header frame, and a called constructor's payload in its own
nominal's, with the exact owner. A call signature's frame must belong to a
function.

- 15 clients exit 42 natively: struct, tuple-struct and tuple-variant fn
  fields built by clients and by provider bodies and constructor calls,
  RawWakerVTable's two `unsafe fn` fields, a generic `fn(T) -> T` field, a
  derived `Copy` struct, `Send + Sync`, and the variance markers, including
  `fn([u8]) -> [u8]`. The unsound narrowing of the contravariant marker is
  rejected, as rustc rejects it.
- Kept fences, each by name and known rejects-valid: higher-ranked,
  early-region and C fn fields. Two former fences now export. Unsafe calls in
  associated fns, with unsafe associated declarations ("source nonordinary
  callable effect/safety/convention requires a wider profile", as Lane D
  found in F), are carried by the associated-unsafe item, and `Table::go`
  exports (amendment 3). Record variants holding fns export since item (i)
  (amendment 4).
- Versions 12 and 10, and the base reader, keep "source shared call type
  requires an actual closed callable frame".
- Among existing inputs the flip survey changes only R2's `field` opponent,
  now exported (pointer-call amendment 2). The 58 compared packets are
  byte-identical.
- **The HR item** must also cover nominal-owned callable frames with bound
  slots, such as `OsFunctions::format_os_error` and `ArgumentType`'s
  formatter, as well as pointer-call frames. The port plan keeps "source
  nominal callable field requires its wider binder profile" for them.
- **Recorded:**
  - an existing header-mapping cost, not scheduled. An empty client's
    header work over N functions, each over a reference to a distinct
    nominal, is 49,821, 54,809 and 65,353 steps for `u8` twins at N = 8, 16
    and 32. With fn fields it is 50,701, 56,569 and 68,873.
    - Each doubling adds about 2.1 times the previous increment in both
      families: 4,988 then 10,544, and 5,868 then 12,304. That is close to
      linear, and the second difference is 568 in both.
    - The fn fields add exactly 110 steps per struct (880, 1,760, 3,520).
    - The cost predates (ii). It missed the declared bound by 504, a failed
      prediction (amendment 2);
  - a pre-existing exporter fence, unassigned: "source anonymous callable
    requires its actual prepared type arena"
    (`sema/crates/authored_export/canonical_nodes/memo_owner.rs:76`). The
    exporter prepares its canonical type arena only on demand:
    - from a function signature with a fn-pointer type
      (`authored_functions.rs:123`);
    - from a transported body's types (`lower/transported_source.rs`);
    - from another analysis that calls `Store::arena_identity`.

    A nominal field's type never prepares it. The shape that hits the fence
    is a provider whose only anonymous callable type is a field's, and in
    which nothing else prepared the arena. One example is
    `pa29/tests/spec/source-nominals/provider.rs`, whose three functions
    mention no fn type and which has no impl body, plus `pub struct Slot {
    pub f: fn(u8) -> i32 }`. It rejects in every profile, on the base pin
    too. After the source-branches provider, the same field exports under
    version 13;
  - rustc 1.98.0 ICEs on a match over an i32 value in the no_core fixtures,
    whose provider gives i32 no Copy; two clients were rewritten before any
    candidate run (amendment 1).

Lane A [census batch 20](parallel/census-batch20.md) repairs census 22's
record. It was qualified and censused on main `4df942467`. Census 23
(`93fb6b89`) checks 42,310 declarations, with none failed or blocked, and
has one record. The crate record is `local_annotations` at
`array/mod.rs:643`, in `<[T; N]>::try_map`: `drain::Drain::new(&mut me,
&mut f)` reports "quantified clause requires a scoped consumer".

- Method lookup now ignores constness, as rustc 1.98's probe does: a
  `[const]` supertrait is a lookup condition only as its ordinary bound, and
  equal to its elaborated ordinary bound it is one condition. `:251` passes.
  Twenty-two PA31 controls were declared before the rustc 1.98.0 oracle;
  all agree, four flip to accept and two `[const]` ambiguity
  accepts-invalids are now E0034-shaped rejections.
- Tracked accepts-invalid (std mode, census batch 20 probes `s6` to `s8`):
  a user trait method named like a prelude comparison method does not
  compete with the prelude's method. `<u8>::cmp(&a, &b)` and
  `<*const V>::cmp(&a, &b)`, and the method-call form `a.cmp(&b)`, with a
  user `trait Cmp { fn cmp(&self, other: &Self) -> u8; }` implemented for
  the type, compile in std mode, where rustc 1.98.0 reports E0034. The root
  is the std prelude model's method candidates, not method applicability:
  batch 20's fix does not change it, and its source-core twin is now
  rejected.
- Tracked rejects-valid (std mode, probes `s2` to `s5`): the std prelude
  model offers no comparison impls for raw pointers. `<*const u8>::cmp`,
  `a.cmp(&b)`, `Ord::cmp(&a, &b)` and `<*const u8>::eq` on `*const u8` are
  rejected, where rustc 1.98.0 accepts them. std needs them.
- Tracked accepts-invalid (probe `b1`): a `[const]` bound in the where
  clause of a method of a non-const impl, such as
  `F: for<'a> [const] Tr<'a>`, is accepted, where rustc 1.98.0 rejects it
  ("`[const]` is not allowed here"). The root is `[const]` placement
  checking, not method lookup.
- Census 23's record 1 is batch 21's lead.

Lane D [B2 F, target-feature rows](parallel/target-feature-rows.md)
carries a free `unsafe fn`'s declared target features under version 13 as
function header bit 3. No closure or table index travels: each consumer
re-proves every imported bit-3 row on its own installed header, resolves the
names against its own feature table and widens only that body's asm CPU
check, to its baseline plus the closure.

- `call`, `generic` and `pointer` call imported target-feature functions
  through their ordinary unsafe signatures and exit 42 natively. The
  imported `keep::<u8>` instance equals the local twin's byte for byte.
- The real `_mm_stream_ps` body now exports with its inline hint, `[sse]`
  and its one template. The cross-crate stream witness runs after
  amendment 5: a client that exports one scalar C function streams through
  the imported body into a 16-byte-aligned local, lowers exactly that
  template and exits 42 natively with the imported `movntps`.
- **Client export fences** (unassigned, next in the queue now that record
  variants have landed). The witness's first client stopped at its own
  library export, and either fence blocks any client library that exports C
  functions over `core::arch` types:
  - `sema/crates/export/records.rs:267`, "nominal shape or representation
    is outside the metadata profile", for a tuple-struct SIMD type such as
    `__m128` in an exported `extern "C"` signature;
  - `sema/crates/export.rs:434`, "exposed type is outside the scalar
    metadata profile", for a raw pointer there.
- Stricter than rustc: safe target-feature functions, trait and trait-impl
  members, non-Rust-ABI and `const` target-feature functions keep named
  fences at export.
- Integrity condition beside S4 (spec.md): a forged bit-3 addition widens
  one imported body's asm CPU check. Any item that admits a register class
  needing a non-baseline feature must cover forged additions first.
- Target features on an unsafe inherent method are carried by the
  associated-unsafe item. F's declared `Lane::get` and its `inherent`
  client were withdrawn, because unsafe inherent methods kept "source
  nonordinary callable effect/safety/convention requires a wider profile"
  at export. `Lane::get` now exports with bit 3 and `inherent.rs` exits 42
  as its client (F's amendment 6).

Lane C [calls through fn pointers](parallel/pointer-calls.md), R2, carries a
call through a closed Rust fn pointer in version-13 exported bodies as a
second shape of value code 30, `(30, [Type, Value, Range, Flag(safety)])`,
with no request row. Each consumer re-proves the signature against the
callee's pointer type node by node, and the safety with direct Call's own
matrix, at validation and again over the rebuilt body. R2 also retires
"source constructor reification requires its shim transport": Q3's re-probe
passed all four steps, because each consumer defines its own constructor
instance.

- 13 clients exit 42 natively. They cover pointer parameters, nested and
  generic calls, a reified local, unsafe pointers in unsafe blocks and bare
  in an unsafe fn, a safe pointer in an unnecessary unsafe block, a safe
  call in an inherent associated fn, the fmt-shaped twin that receives its
  formatter as a parameter, and three reified constructors.
- Kept fences, each by name: higher-ranked and C pointers, and const fns,
  which rustc also rejects. An unsafe call in an associated fn exports since
  the associated-unsafe item (amendment 3), a pointer stored in a struct
  field since (ii), and one in a record variant's field since (i)
  (amendment 5).
- Versions 12 and 10 keep the direct-call text. The base2 reader rejects the
  node with "body IR value node has malformed operands", which an
  operand-swap forgery also gets.
- Among existing inputs, the flip survey changes only R1's four amended
  opponents: `indirect` and the struct, variant and generic constructors.
- **The fmt chain.** core's `ArgumentType` stores its formatter in a record
  variant's fn-pointer field, so it needs both of these, ahead of HR/C. R2
  alone does not unblock it.
  - (i) Record variants: done, Lane D's [record
    variants](parallel/record-variants.md). With (ii), a record variant's
    zero-slot fn field exports; `ArgumentType`'s formatter is higher-ranked,
    so it waits for HR.
  - (ii) Nominal callable fields: `frame_owners` requires a function header
    to own each callable-type binder ("source shared call type requires an
    actual closed callable frame"), and the port plan keeps "source nominal
    callable field requires its wider binder profile". (ii) now carries
    zero-slot fields (see above); fields with bound slots wait for HR.
- **Associated unsafe calls.** "source unsafe associated Calls require their
  wider actual profile" widened for direct and pointer calls together under
  version 13, with unsafe associated declarations, in the associated-unsafe
  item (see above).
- Failed predictions: rustc 1.98.0 rejects the const-fn opponent with the
  unnumbered "function pointer calls are not allowed in constant functions",
  not the declared E0015. A forged constructor pointer with a changed payload
  stops at the formation check first; with its formation input it reaches the
  re-proof (outcomes amendment 1).

- A member's conformance contract environment now certifies its copies of

Lane D [B2 S, SIMD header rows](parallel/simd-header-rows.md) carries
`#[repr(simd)]` structs under version 13 as nominal header representation
byte 3. No lane, count or layout fact travels: each consumer maps the byte to
its own SIMD representation, re-runs the declaration and layout checks over
its own field, and re-proves an imported asm body's `xmm_reg` operand.

- The `store` client lowers `movups [{p}],{a}` over an imported
  `V([u32; 4])` and exits 42 natively. Forging `V`'s byte to 0 fails that
  re-proof by name.
- Known stricter-than-rustc item (S3): a SIMD struct with any type, const or
  lifetime parameter fails at export with "source generic SIMD record
  requires its wider complete contract", which rustc 1.98.0 accepts. It
  waits for a lane proof at each consumer's instantiation.
- Packed and aligned SIMD structs keep the representation fence, and lane
  limits keep theirs. Both old readers reject the packet by name, and
  existing v13 packets are byte-identical.
- Integrity limit (S4): without asm, a 3-to-0 rewrite is ABI-visible and
  undetected, like the transparent 2-to-0 rewrite. It is left for a later
  ABI digest.
- F followed: the stream witness now exports `_mm_stream_ps` (see F's
  entry above).

- Older profiles keep the type-form text, the comment-fence reader rejects

Lane C [scheduling-context fence](parallel/scheduling-context.md) removes
"implementation validation has no source scheduling context" for imported
headers. A source client with no source core, outside selected mode, now
validates an imported impl when an executable demand during declaration
preparation first needs it. It uses the eager walk's own conformance, and the
walk then skips only that owner's conformance.

- 14 rustc-accepted clients that the base pin rejected at the fence are
  accepted and exit 42 natively, and 17 accepted clients are unchanged.
  The named-constants client `const-call` is accepted (its amendment 9).
  Against the base pin, the flip survey changes only `const-call`.
- The overlap control is rejected before and after, in a different order: the
  base pin reports the fence, and the candidate reports the overlap text.
- Selected mode keeps the base path and text, because it installs no constant
  executor. Completing imported conformance through its selected dependency
  on demand becomes reachable only if selected mode gains a constant executor.
- Known rejects-valid: a client-local Copy impl reached through an imported
  generic const fn during declaration preparation reports "client
  implementation validation before the eager walk requires a source
  scheduling context". Its follow-up is an early scheduler for client source
  headers outside a source core.
- An imported endpoint of the client's specialization relations stops with
  "imported implementation validation before its specialization conformance
  order". No real input reaches it, and a private test forges each endpoint.
- Efficiency item, an untraced hypothesis: an imported body's Copy goal reads
  the coarse primitive `heads` bucket, so a client demands every
  primitive-headed Copy impl (14 for the scaling provider), while a local
  body's resolved goal reads its exact `closed` bucket. Each such impl is one
  the eager walk validates anyway.
- Existing fence, independent of this item: a client const evaluation of an
  imported Copy-bounded generic const fn stops at "defining cleanup requires
  its actual language declaration" when the provider declares no Destruct
  language item. The branch provider's 57 lines lack it, and core declares
  it.
- Lane C's next item is R2, with `value_rows.rs`'s `emit()` factored first.

Lane A [census batch 18](parallel/census-batch18.md) repairs census 20's
record and its lookahead. It was qualified and censused on main

failed and one blocked. Its crate record is the `next_back` method of the
same `DoubleEndedIterator for RSplit` impl (`str/iter.rs:600`):
"associated type requires an unambiguous validated implementation".

- In a census lookahead after unfinished impl validation, a validated

- An elaborated supertrait premise inherits a certified declared bound's
  certificate when both have the same binder shape and subject and its

  controls were declared before the rustc 1.98.0 oracle; all statuses
  agree.
- Stricter than rustc under D4: `elaborated-projection-argument` (rustc 0,

  `DoubleKeyed<'a, P::Key>` bound elaborates to `Keyed<'a, P::Key>`, whose

- Census 21's record 1 predates the batch: a written premise fails the same
  way on census 20's pin. It is batch 19's lead.

Lane C [named constants](parallel/named-constants.md), 5b-1, exports named
const items of primitive and unit types under version 13, at module scope and

it as a completed definition.

- Twelve clients exit 42 natively. They cover runtime reads, a client const,

- Kept fences, each by name: aggregate, reference and pointer values,

- Failed prediction, amendment 5: `const-call`, client const evaluation of the

Lane D [B2 A, asm transport](parallel/asm-transport.md) exports version 13
bodies holding the inline assembly forms of B1's initial profile: raw-pointer
`reg` inputs, Intel templates without modifiers, and `nostack` and
`preserves_flags`. They travel as value code 0 and a cold `Original.assembly`
column.

- Each consumer re-validates the columns before any proof: unsafe context,
  operand classes and types, placeholders and SSA domination. It rebuilds the
  body's own payload and lowers one template per emitted instance through
  B1's path.
- A pa41 provider exports, and its two clients exit 42 natively, among them
  two instances of one imported generic asm body. Two opponents fail with
  their local twins' texts.
- Each of the 15 generated controls outside the profile fails at export with
  its B1 lowering text, from one shared table. Older profiles keep their

  existing v13 packets are byte-identical.
- Two failed predictions are kept with their amendments: the provider's
  packet carries Lane B's macro envelope for the foundation's builtin
  `arch::asm`, and the pinned backend's assembler has no `/* */` comments.
- Comment fence (the follow-up before S): `/* */` and `//` templates fail
  with "inline assembly comments other than # are outside the initial
  assembly profile". The check runs after the modifiers, at lowering, at
  export and in every v13 reader. It is lexical over each instruction's
  joined templates, so `//` and `/*` inside a quoted directive string still
  lower and export. Two opponents, two export controls and forged rows 23
  and 24 fail with that text.
- Backend capability note: the pinned CPPGM assembler reads `#` line comments
  only. A declared probe showed that it rejects `//` ("assembly line 1:
  expected module assembly identifier") as it rejects `/* */`, and that it
  rejects a quoted `.ascii` directive in inline assembly ("assembly line 2:
  expected assembly integer"). rustc 1.98.0 accepts all three. RustGM fences
  the two comment forms by name; extending the backend is the user's
  decision, and cppgm-extended is unchanged.
- Open: `xmm_reg` inputs wait for S and T's vector type rows. The stream
  witness still stops at `__m128`'s `repr(simd)`.

Lane B [premise sides](parallel/negative-premise-sides.md) negates each
negative coherence condition under the other header's premises only, as
rustc 1.98's `impl_intersection_has_negative_obligation` does, on main
`28e2b19fb`:

- A rigid intersection parameter is Sized or `MetaSized` only through the
  other header's Sized and `MetaSized` premises. This is the placeholder
  rustc plugs in after equating the headers. The declaration of the
  representative `freeze` chose is no longer read.
- A negative payment pays Sized debts only from the other header's Sized
  premises, now including source `Sized` clauses, written or elaborated (`U:
  Copy`). Its lifetime obligations assume only that header's written
  type-outlives premises, with no formed types and no region-region bound.
  An unpaid obligation means "not disjoint" (the overlap text), not "required
  lifetime does not outlive its target".
- Repaired: `sized-premise-side`, the accepts-invalid recorded below.
  Repaired: `sized-client-side`, a rejects-valid on main at `dcb956cf8`. When
  the blanket's `?Sized` `V` was the representative, the client's Sized `U`
  could not pay `T: Sized`. It is accepted, as rustc accepts it.
- 21 PA31 controls (`negative-premise-sides`) were declared before their
  rustc 1.98.0 oracle. With `negative-metasized`'s two side twins, 22 of 23
  match rustc, and each swapped pair has its twin's outcome. Five declared
  outcomes were wrong predictions and are amended beside their declarations.
- The flip survey over 336 negative and strict coherence fixtures has no
  accepted-to-rejected flip. One rejected case changes from the region text
  to the overlap text. The analysis-only census lookahead's `census.tsv` is
  byte-identical to the base's.
- Fixed by the [nested-sizedness item](parallel/nested-sizedness.md): the
  recorded accepts-invalid `nested-premise-side`. A non-parameter type whose
  sizedness depends on a rigid parameter, such as `Wrap<V>: Sized` for
  `struct Wrap<X: ?Sized>(X)`, is now Sized by rustc's `sized_conditions`
  under the other header's Sized premises. The shape walk, size query and
  payment no longer read the representative's declaration. Both twins are
  rejected, as rustc rejects them with E0119. 26 new twins were declared
  before their rustc 1.98.0 oracle. Of the 47 cases, 43 match rustc, and the
  symmetry invariant has no exemption. The flip survey and the census
  lookahead are byte-identical to the base.
- Known rejects-valid until item B, which repairs it: `nested-projected-size`
  and its swapped twin, which rustc accepts. A projected-size nominal's size
  came from a nested trait proof without the header premises, so a named
  fence rejected it in both orders.
- Open rejects-valid: the `probe-trait-premise` pair, which rustc accepts. It
  is rejected in both orders because a negative impl's trait condition is not
  assumed from the other header's trait premises.

Lane C [fn-item reification](parallel/fn-reification.md), R1, transports a fn

each consumer re-proves the item's applied signature against the pointer,
with `unsafe` added but never removed.

- Nine clients exit 42 natively. They cover:

Lane B [MetaSized premises](parallel/negative-metasized.md) proves a
negative impl's `MetaSized` condition from the other header's premises, as
rustc 1.98's `impl_intersection_has_negative_obligation` does, on main
`68c26f42a`:

- A negative coherence query for one header's condition assumes the other
  header's `MetaSized` premises: its Sized parameters, its Sized, Copy and
  Clone subjects and its `MetaSized` clauses, written, defaulted from
  `?Sized` or implied by a supertrait. A proof from them stays a debt that
  only that set pays. So `impl<T: ?Sized> !Seal for &T` makes a blanket over
  `T: Seal` disjoint from `impl<U: ?Sized> Holds<&U> for Local`.
- 13 PA31 controls were declared before their rustc 1.98.0 oracle, and
  rustc gives every declared outcome. This compiler matches rustc on 12;
  `sized-premise-side` is the recorded accepts-invalid below. One declared
  RustGM outcome was a wrong prediction: `supertrait-implied`, declared
  fenced, is accepted, as rustc accepts it.
- C3's `negative-coherence` client and C3b's `negative-metasized-param`
  control, which rustc accepts, are amended from recorded gaps to accepted.
- Repaired by Lane B's [premise sides](parallel/negative-premise-sides.md)
  (above); kept as recorded (accepts-invalid): negative coherence still pays
  a Sized debt from both headers' Sized premises. `overlap/inputs.rs` gathers
  both headers' Sized parameters and Sized, Copy and Clone subjects into one
  `sized` set, and `freeze` makes the first header's binding the rigid
  representative, so the negated clause's own impl can prove its Sized
  condition. rustc assumes only `param_env(impl1)` while negating impl2's
  clauses. Repro: `sized-premise-side`, which rustc rejects with E0119 and
  this compiler accepts. `prove` also assumes both headers' regions,
  outlives and formed types; the next item's lifetime-premise twin checks
  that side.

Lane C [auto traits](parallel/auto-traits.md), C2b, forms each imported

- An uncharged walk pays the installed header budget exactly the

Six clients exit 42 natively. The flip survey changes only the new packet:
its seven imported opponents go from accepted to rejected. Every other run is

Lane C [auto traits](parallel/auto-traits.md), C3b, proves coherence absence
for the crate whose coherence it proves, as rustc 1.98's
`trait_ref_is_knowable` does. There is no wire change:

- A consumer re-proves a pair imported from one packet from that packet's
  perspective, over what the packet could see. Core's
  `impl<T: Error + ?Sized> Error for &T` beside `impl !Error for &str`, which
  exports but was rejected by every consumer, is now accepted: C3's generated
  pair control and the new `knowable-absence` packet.
- A client's own pairs are proved for the client. An imported trait's goal is
  knowable when the client owns an input type, through `&`, `&mut` and
  `#[fundamental]` nominals. So `impl base::Error for &Local {}` beside the
  blanket `Error for &T` is accepted, as rustc accepts it.

Six clients exit 42 natively. Seven opponents are rejected as rustc rejects
them, each at its own impl: five E0119 cases whose missing impl another crate
could add, and two orphan-rule cases.

The candidate found that coherence never read an imported impl's
where-clauses, so every consumer re-proved imported pairs with no conditions.
It now reads the same imported original inputs selection does.

- Open in this item:
  - **Diagnostic quality.** An imported pair's coherence failure is unlocated
    (`input:`), so a dishonest pair in a forged packet would still be
    reported that way. It is not fixed in C3b.
  - **Conservative limits.** A pair across two packets is proved for the
    consumer until multi-packet; `dyn` and foreign types are never owned
    inputs; an imported `#[fundamental]` nominal is never peeled, since a
    packet cannot carry one.
  - The C3 entry's other items stand. The lifetime-capturing trait-impl
    method blocker, scheduled after C3b, stays unowned.

Lane C [auto traits](parallel/auto-traits.md), third commit (C3), transports
unsafe traits and every trait impl's own safety and polarity in version 13,
with no new tag, bit or row:

- A non-auto trait header may carry `UNSAFE`, and any trait impl may carry

Six clients exit 42 natively, `negative-coherence` since Lane B's

    implemented". Core's `DerefMut for &mut T` and `Error for &E` need it.
    Scheduled after C3b; a candidate to fold into the multi-packet item.
  - **Repaired: negative coherence over a `?Sized` negative impl** (Lane B,
    [MetaSized premises](parallel/negative-metasized.md)). A negative
    coherence query now assumes the other header's own `MetaSized` premises,
    so the first two rows below are accepted. Before, `pay_negative`
    (`overlap/inputs.rs`) pays only Sized premises, so a `?Sized` (MetaSized)
    negative impl's premise is never paid from the intersection's own
    MetaSized assumption. It is independent of transport: with every item
    local, the C3 provider's foundation (its lines 2-58) plus this line is
    rejected with "overlapping trait implementation headers require coherence
    proof", where rustc 1.98.0 accepts it:

    ```rust
    pub trait A {} impl<T: ?Sized> !A for &T {} pub trait B {} impl<T: A> B for T {} impl<T: ?Sized> B for &T {}
    ```

    | Negative impl's `T` | `B for &T`'s `T` | rustc 1.98.0 | RustGM |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | `?Sized` | `?Sized` | accepted | accepted (was overlap text) |
    | `MetaSized` | `MetaSized` | accepted | accepted (was overlap text) |
    | `PointeeSized` | `?Sized` | accepted | accepted |
    | `PointeeSized` | `PointeeSized` | accepted | accepted |
    | `Sized` | `Sized` | accepted | accepted |

    C3's `negative-coherence` client, declared rejected with the overlap
    text, is amended to accepted and exits 42.
  - **`may_dangle`**, under the existing open item (frontend dropck; it
    blocks alloc, not core). It has three parts: resolving the attribute
    (`#[may_dangle]` is "unresolved attribute macro is not in path scope"
    before export), the unsafe-Drop safety rule, and the dropck opt-out. No
    fence is added.
  - C2b and C2c follow C3b, as before.

Lane B [shim glob rows and `compiler_builtins`](parallel/shim-globs-builtins.md)
lets crates whose `core` is the `rustc_std_workspace_core` shim resolve

  is `#![compiler_builtins]`. It is written only when a flag is set, so other

  `#![compiler_builtins]` needs a flagged artifact in its inventory.
  Otherwise it fails with rustc's E0463 text, or by name when `--extern
  compiler_builtins=` names an unflagged crate.

Lane C [transparent record headers](parallel/transparent-records.md) is

Lane C [shared statics require Sync](parallel/shared-static-sync.md) is
joined on main. It fixes a false acceptance: main accepted a
source-client `static` with no `sync` lang item, and rustc rejects it. A

item (rustc's "requires `sync` lang_item") and a proof that its type

shared static against a packet core.
